## P15.6 owner closure — local architecture ready (2026-10-02)

**P15.6 LOCAL ARCHITECTURE = READY FOR OWNER REVIEW.** Starting and current HEAD: `f66b4aff8c593340490b5e30c0b6123e0f0edeb7` on main. Objective: close the owner's local name-first, governed-chat, and deterministic graduation-comparison blockers while preserving inherited P15.6 work. This checkpoint supersedes the historical October 1 blocker assessment below; it does not promote any canonical WC status.

**Name-first coverage:** Student catalog/progress/eligibility/recommendation/planner/roadmap/report/degree-path, advisor clarification, decision-history course evidence, modeled plan-transition, offering/intelligence, and institutional query/capacity/change-impact surfaces now prefer the canonical localized name and retain the stable code second. Arabic/English alternate-language and unavailable-name fallback are explicit. Batched owner/analyst-authorized catalog maps are indexed by ID/code, reject foreign scope, and avoid per-course network calls; general chat does not trigger the clarification catalog fetch. Historical evidence IDs and domain fingerprints are not rewritten. Repository searches for raw course-code renderings were inspected in context; remaining matches are machine identities, keys, input values with name previews, or the shared display component. No known code-only human-facing gap remains on reviewed surfaces where canonical names exist. Academic graph/trace labels use available catalog metadata without changing rule outcomes.

**Chat and P14 governance:** Server-backed conversation architecture persists threads/messages and explicit preference history, not localStorage. Refresh/logout/archive/inactivity do not delete chats. Owner + institution + thread scope is checked before read/archive/export/deletion requests. P14 export allowlists owned metadata/messages/current preferences, includes archived chats, excludes hidden/provider fields, and declares bounded truncation (500 threads/1,000 messages). No stored model summary is invented. Chat deletion is classified DELETABLE_OPTIONAL_DATA and routed to human review of the exact owned thread, not destructive deletion. Retention is USER_OWNED_CONVERSATION_DATA / RETENTION_POLICY_NOT_VERIFIED; no legal period is invented. P14 remains local/unconfigured for production governance. Latest explicit regular/summer/pace preferences are resolved through four bounded latest-key reads; arbitrary academic keys are excluded. The explicit poisoned-memory test proves GPA/grade/credit/prerequisite claims leave the deterministic result and repository state unchanged with zero write calls.

**Graduation comparison and semester balance:** FASTEST, BALANCED, and LOWER_LOAD reuse the deterministic credit simulator. Presets are regular 12/15/18 and summer off/3/6/9; validated custom preferences add at most 20 total scenarios. Stable tie-breaking and preference matching expose time/load tradeoffs, never a universally best plan. BALANCED combines duration/load penalties, stated preferences, and available P15.5 current-eligible workload risk; it does not invent future course difficulty. Summer enabled is 3–9, disabled is 0. Student-supplied start and First/Second/Summer sequence are MODELED_ACADEMIC_CALENDAR, not an official graduation date or prerequisite/offerings guarantee. Stored preferences prefill but do not overwrite subsequent manual choices; changed inputs invalidate stale comparison responses. The existing course-aware path remains separate. Planner default <=2 MEMORIZATION_HEAVY and explicit BALANCE_CONSTRAINT_RELAXATION_REQUIRED remain, with overload names/codes and deterministic eligibility/credits preserved.

**Migration/security review:** The single existing new migration `20261001200504_add_student_conversation_history.sql` is MIGRATION_CREATED_NOT_APPLIED. Static review checked owner/institution indexes, composite thread/message FK, trigger scope, authenticated owner-only SELECT RLS and server-only writes. Qualified the message policy's outer institution column and changed profile deletion FK to RESTRICT so profile deletion cannot silently cascade-delete chats; auth-user lifecycle FK remains explicit. No duplicate migration, client service-role key, broad authenticated write policy, provider payload logging, or academic-state write path was added. Live enforcement is not inferred from mocked/static tests. Secret/private-key/token and absolute machine-path scan of changed source/test/migration files returned zero pattern matches. Supabase configuration is identical to HEAD. Both protected SQL backups remain untracked and were not opened or modified.

## P15.6 course UX, durable chat, credit timeline, and plan balance (2026-10-01)

**Objective/start:** Owner-requested pre-P16 slice. Starting `main`/`origin/main` SHA `f66b4aff8c593340490b5e30c0b6123e0f0edeb7`. Initial worktree contained only the two protected untracked `production_pre_p8_replay_*.sql` backups; neither was opened or changed. Audited existing course identity, student/advisor flows, planner/degree-path engines, P14 privacy architecture, and migrations before extending them. No stage, commit, push, deployment, production write, Supabase reset, migration application, or final comprehensive regression occurred.

**Implementation:** Reusable bilingual `CourseIdentity` makes canonical Arabic/English names primary and stable codes secondary on the student catalog, progress, eligibility, recommendations, planner, roadmap, degree path, and report pages where names are supplied. Batch catalog/attempt/progress/recommendation fields carry display metadata without changing academic IDs, joins, rules, or authorization. Advisor explanation guidance prefers canonical name plus code, but the LLM must not invent a missing name. Some code-only surfaces with no name metadata, including parts of advisor clarification, decision/What-If traces, and institutional tools, remain to be audited before claiming global name-first coverage.

No existing durable chat table/repository was found. The **created but unapplied** `20261001200504_add_student_conversation_history.sql` migration defines threads, messages, preference history, owner/institution/profile constraints, service-role-only writes, authenticated owner-scoped read RLS, and scope triggers. The authenticated conversation API derives owner and institution server-side, supports list/create/reopen/continue/rename/archive, and returns 404 for foreign/guessed threads. Service-role queries use exact owner and institution filters plus response-scope checks. User text persists before advisor processing; assistant text persists after the deterministic decision/explanation. A storage failure returns a redacted 503, not a transcript. The browser uses this server-backed API instead of localStorage. Explicit planning preferences supersede earlier values by append-only history, and bounded recent text plus a bounded preference summary are marked non-authoritative in advisor context. No raw chat, hidden reasoning, or secret is intentionally logged. P14 export/deletion/retention integration is **not yet implemented**; archive is not deletion.

The separate deterministic credit-only timeline accepts regular planning loads including 12/15/18, optional summer 3–9 (0 when disabled), a student-supplied starting term/year, and emits exact per-term arithmetic, partial final term, provenance warnings, and no course-feasibility guarantee. It does not claim an authoritative institutional calendar or actual graduation date; prerequisites, offerings/capacity, failures, and the 90-credit AI Project rule can change it. The existing course-aware degree path remains separate. The planner now supplies a versioned `MODELED_COURSE_LOAD_PROFILE_V1` for each loaded Plan 12 course, explicitly falls back to unknown elsewhere, and filters >2 memorization-heavy courses by default. Explicit relaxation exposes extra heavy-course codes and retains deterministic eligibility/credit constraints; P15.5 adaptive rank remains a tie-breaker. The modeled classification is not an institution-verified workload survey.

**Verification so far:** Combined focused/affected backend matrix: **834 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped, 2 dependency deprecation warnings, 9.43s** (planner, advisor, degree path, catalog, eligibility, progress, recommendations, and P15.6 tests). Focused frontend portal/course-identity/recommendations/roadmap-report: **31 passed, 0 failed**; one pre-existing React `act` warning. Typecheck passed. Scoped ESLint: **0 errors, 27 warnings** in touched files, primarily pre-existing unused imports/effect warnings. `git diff --check` and final status are recorded at the final checkpoint. A broader affected backend run initially found an optional planner-argument test-double compatibility regression and a new warning-string assertion; both were corrected and rerun green. An older advisor input-shape assertion was updated for the optional bounded context; its rerun passed. The frontend portal suite initially found stale generic mocks and first-form selectors after adding the credit form; corrected mocks/selectors and malformed-response guard, then all 23 portal tests passed. No live Supabase/DB/RLS migration or two-student persistence proof is claimed.

**Acceptance/remaining problems:** **P15.6 LOCAL VERDICT = IMPLEMENTATION BLOCKERS REMAIN** for the owner's full standard. Global course-name-first coverage across remaining code-only advisor/trace/institutional surfaces is incomplete; live migration/RLS and actual cross-device persistence are unverified; P14 governed export/deletion/retention is not wired to chat; authoritative institutional calendar and course-aware feasibility are unavailable; multi-option `FASTEST`/`BALANCED`/`LOWER_LOAD` tradeoff comparison is not implemented. These must not be presented as production-validated. Next exact starting point: complete remaining name-bearing API/display surfaces and advisor candidate labels, add real local database/RLS owner/tenant migration tests without applying to production, integrate governed P14 chat export/deletion policy, and verify authoritative calendar/feasibility or preserve explicit modeled-only labels. Retain all current work unstaged for owner review; recommended eventual message: `feat(student-experience): persist chat and improve academic planning`.

## P15.5 owner-decision local closure (2026-10-01)

**Objective/start:** Close only the current uncommitted P15.5 slice after explicit owner decisions. Starting `main` SHA = `origin/main` = `51ea8489b13e4eacbed3fc27b789c9c5e24d7706`; the prior P15.5 checkpoint and its 29 project changes were preserved. The two protected untracked P8 SQL backups were not opened or modified. No stage, commit, push, deploy, migration, Local Supabase start/reset, or comprehensive regression occurred.

**Owner-defined scope and implementation:** “Every course” now means every active course in the currently supported canonical study-plan catalog, not unrelated external university courses. The Plan 12 seed currently enumerates **68** courses; the focused coverage test reads that canonical seed dynamically, compares the entire discovered set to the generated difficulty set, validates general/personal score/level/provenance/model version for each course, and verifies that an added supported course also gets an explicitly labeled fallback. The batch model already consumes the loaded plan catalog rather than a fixed 68-course list, so no per-course request or silent null difficulty is introduced. An explicit fixture-only `MODELED_GRADE_SCALE_V1` / `SYNTHETIC_ONLY` scale exercises verified-grade student variation. With no approved real grade scale, the production path has no configured `GradePolicy`: grade mastery/strength/weakness is excluded, the profile reports `UNVERIFIED_GRADE_SCALE`, and each personal estimate has `LOW` confidence, modeled structural-fallback provenance, and `INSUFFICIENT_VERIFIED_GRADE_EVIDENCE` plus `UNVERIFIED_GRADE_SCALE` reasons. Stale-source evidence produces `STALE_INPUT` and low confidence. The student-facing difficulty component exposes personal provenance; if the batch read fails, recommendations explicitly say the displayed order is generic and every card shows difficulty unavailable rather than silently omitting it. The exact Plan 12 project codes `1505467`/`1505468` retain their additive `>=90` passed-plan-credit rule; a new focused pipeline test proves the project stays out of ranked eligible courses before 90 and that Project 2 still needs Project 1.

**Verification:** Focused P15.5/backend directly affected matrix: **563 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped, 2 existing dependency deprecation warnings in 6.98s**. Focused frontend: **13 passed, 0 failed** (including adaptive-present and generic-fallback recommendation presentation); typecheck passed; scoped ESLint passed with **0 errors and 14 pre-existing warnings**. `git diff --check` is checked again in the final repository check. No live DB/RLS integration is claimed. No LLM path can change the deterministic rule, eligible set, rank, GPA, grade, or skill output; the new response is a read-only owner-derived batch with private/no-store semantics and no per-course fetch. No cross-student cache was added.

**Acceptance and deferrals:** By explicit owner decision, **P15.5 LOCAL ARCHITECTURE = IMPLEMENTED / READY TO STAGE FOR OWNER REVIEW**. **REAL INSTITUTIONAL GRADE-SCALE CALIBRATION = EXTERNAL_DEPENDENCY / NOT VERIFIED**; **REAL GRADE-DRIVEN PRODUCTION PERSONALIZATION = NOT VERIFIED**; **LIVE DB/RLS = DEFERRED_TO_POST_P16_FINAL_VALIDATION**. Advisor adaptive UI is future/optional, not a local gate. This local result is not production institutional validation, a trained outcome model, or official registration approval. No canonical WC status is promoted. Next start: owner stage review of this complete uncommitted slice; preserve protected backups, then perform post-P16 comprehensive validation and external grade-scale/RLS checks. Recommended commit message after separate approval: `feat(student-intelligence): add adaptive course difficulty and recommendations`.

## P15.5 student intelligence and adaptive course recommendation (2026-10-01)

**Objective/start:** Owner-requested pre-P16 P15.5 slice from `main` HEAD = `origin/main` = `51ea8489b13e4eacbed3fc27b789c9c5e24d7706`. Initial status contained only the two protected untracked P8 SQL backups; they were never opened or changed. Audited canonical attempt/grade/GPA records, eligibility, progress, catalog, recommendations, planner, degree path, roadmap, P11 skill foundations, student UI, and security boundaries before implementation.

**Implementation:** Exact Plan 12 AI Project `1505467` and `1505468` receive an additive `>=90` passed listed-plan credit rule and versioned Arabic/English trace; passed attempts count once, while attempted, planned, and reported-only credit figures do not authorize the rule. Missing complete credit evidence fails to review. The owner-only batch endpoint derives institution and student identity server-side, rejects query spoofing, sets `private, no-store`, and returns versioned source/eligible-set fingerprints, canonical reported GPA, modeled completed credits, verified-grade-only skills and strengths/weaknesses, general and personal course difficulty, and eligible-only adaptive recommendations. Explicit institution/plan-bound grade normalization is a policy boundary; **no production-approved grade scale is configured**, so current real unverified grades are never interpreted as mastery. Academic stage and source freshness are unknown unless evidenced. General difficulty is a structural model with labeled fallback, not institutional historical statistics. Planner uses adaptive scores only inside the existing eligible candidate set and preserves deterministic constraints. Student roadmap, eligibility, planner, degree path, and recommendation surfaces display bilingual modeled difficulty, confidence, and the project-credit trace; recommendations show an academic summary. No LLM determines academic facts, no write path/migration was added, and no advisor authorization route was changed.

**Verification:** Directly affected backend suites: **561 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped, 2 dependency deprecation warnings, 7.30s**. Focused frontend tests: **11 passed, 0 failed**; TypeScript typecheck passed. Scoped ESLint had **0 errors, 14 existing-style warnings** (unused imports/effect warnings in touched pre-existing pages). `git diff --check` passed before this documentation update and is rerun at final checkpoint. A catalog-repository mock initially failed because its fixture omitted newly selected `credit_hours`; the fixture was corrected and the expanded run passed. A roadmap mock initially lacked the new batch API and was updated; focused rerun passed. No full P1–P16 regression, Local Supabase/DB/RLS validation, production smoke, migration, deployment, staging, commit, or push was run.

**Limits/acceptance/next:** Static Plan 12 seed enumeration proves modeled difficulty for all seed-listed courses in-memory; it does not prove every live active course outside the loaded student's plan, live university catalog coverage, or production DB/RLS behavior. Without an institution-approved versioned grade policy and verified transcript-grade provenance, production personal difficulty remains low-confidence and cannot demonstrate grade-driven student variation (focused explicit-policy tests do demonstrate the model). There is no trained historical outcome data, advisor-facing adaptive view, or official registration decision. **P15.5 LOCAL VERDICT = IMPLEMENTATION BLOCKERS REMAIN** for the owner's full every-catalog-course and production-grade personalization claims. ## P10 local acceptance-blocker closure (2026-09-30 to 2026-10-01)

**Objective/start:** Close only the three owner-identified local P10 acceptance blockers: runnable fictional tenant, governed modeled-curriculum boundary, and bounded deterministic sensitivity. No backup was opened or changed; no stage, commit, push, deployment, production migration, or production write occurred.

**Implementation/security:** `tests/p10_synthetic_fixture.py` creates a fixed, explicitly fictional university/faculty/major/plan/group/four-course/period only against loopback Local Supabase and a minimal second fictional catalog/plan identity solely as a cross-tenant isolation sentinel. It validates existing fixed rows for idempotency and creates ephemeral real Auth student/analyst/foreign-student principals. Both profiles and the analyst membership are removed before Auth teardown; a separately authorized local DB reset removes fixed catalog/period rows if desired. The deterministic development/test provider supplies multi-section, full/open/unknown-capacity, conflict, modality/location, stale, and synthetic-provenance variants; production continues to use an unavailable provider. Real JWT-backed routes verify student owner scope, analyst membership, cross-tenant non-disclosure, stale and unavailable provider states, cross-scope provider failure, replay, and before/after immutable provider fingerprint. The same authenticated analyst route also consumes actual P6 pure-aggregator outputs for allowed two-owner and suppressed one-owner synthetic demand, including simulation/sensitivity privacy checks; those input intents exist in memory only. No synthetic registration-intent revision is persisted because P6 intent records are intentionally immutable and would defeat safe teardown.

**Modeled boundary:** Curriculum assumptions are closed to `COURSE_AVAILABLE_IN_MODELED_PERIOD` and `COURSE_NOT_AVAILABLE_IN_MODELED_PERIOD` for courses already present in the base offering snapshot. They only restore/remove known base sections in an in-memory modeled supply snapshot; they do not edit plans, prerequisites, requirements, academic decisions, or official schedules. Assumptions carry fixed modeled provenance in canonical scenario fingerprints. The sensitivity API and pure engine allow only capacity, demand delta, or added-section-count sweeps, at most 11 deterministic points, with explicit ranges and no selection, probability, prediction, or operational write. Suppressed demand/gaps and unknown/stale supply remain unknown. The institutional page exposes a three-point modeled capacity sweep. Identical synthetic replay produces an identical fingerprint and changed assumptions produce different fingerprints; this is not real backtesting.

## P9 local acceptance-blocker closure — owner review (2026-09-30)

**Objective and start:** Close only WC-006 modeled planned overlay and deterministic critical-path representation, WC-047 immutable/version-aware modeled report, and WC-048 local accessibility/PWA verification. Started from `main` HEAD = `origin/main` = `d429dc07cbb4806fb43f13d47f31006a6b597040` with the uncommitted P9 checkpoint below and two protected untracked `production_pre_p8_replay_*.sql` backups. Neither backup was opened, changed, nor staged. No commit, push, deployment, production migration, or production write.

**Implementation:** Base `GET /api/v1/me/academic-roadmap` remains owner-derived, read-only, and free of automatic degree-path calculation (one owner read, four parallel plan-scoped catalog reads). A separate explicit authenticated `POST /api/v1/me/academic-roadmap/modeled-path` reuses the bounded deterministic degree-path service, shared CPU limiter, timeout, and disconnect cancellation. Its first ranked path becomes a server-only semester/order overlay on the captured owner/plan inputs; stale plan/fingerprint, duplicate, unavailable, and missing-policy overlays fail closed. The base roadmap shows no PLANNED nodes and explains the absent model; generated nodes are explicitly modeled, not registered/scheduled. Structural critical path is defined as every unfinished required plan course on a globally longest verified single-option prerequisite chain, excluding elective/OR/corequisite/unresolved edges, with chain length, downstream required codes, reason, and evidence chain. It makes no calendar/guaranteed-delay prediction.

**Report and source contract:** `GET /api/v1/me/academic-report` freezes one owner-scoped roadmap into a frozen report payload with P9 schema/marker, plan/source/version metadata, input fingerprint, deterministic content fingerprint, source timestamp, generated timestamp, course-state sections, and limits. Canonical content excludes generated time, so equivalent inputs reproduce the same fingerprint. The protected Arabic/English screen and print views use one authenticated `no-store` payload, and the API sends `Cache-Control: private, no-store`. No student owner identifier, raw attempt data, secret, predicted graduation date, official seal, or private offline cache is exported. This is a captured application-input snapshot, not a transactionally frozen database snapshot or an official transcript.

**Accessibility/PWA local evidence and limits:** Roadmap list/buttons expose text status, prerequisites, modeled semester/order, critical evidence, keyboard selection and live details; Arabic RTL and English LTR are tested. Report and roadmap loading/error regions announce state. Planner/eligibility error and loading regions gained semantic announcements; advisor chat gained a named textbox and live log. Global focus-visible outline and reduced-motion rules are present. Priority pages were statically inspected for headings, labels, and regions; that inspection is not a screen-reader or full WCAG audit. Production build generated `/manifest.webmanifest`; local HTTP returned 200 with name, short name, `/` start/scope, standalone display, Arabic RTL, theme, and icon; favicon HTTP returned 200 and the ICO header contains 16/32/48/256-square images. In-app browser discovery returned no available browser, so responsive/device installation, screen-reader behavior, automated contrast, and manual print fidelity remain unverified. Local PWA contract verified; device installability requires device validation. Local accessibility checks are not formal WCAG certification.

**Implementation checkpoint:** A new owner-derived `GET /api/v1/me/academic-roadmap` projects plan progress and verified prerequisite decisions from existing deterministic engines. It performs one student state load and four plan-scoped catalog reads, then in-memory course evaluation; it does not launch degree-path search. Cross-catalog plan/code mismatches fail closed. Course states include completed, in progress, eligible, blocked, and review required. `PLANNED` is supported only by the pure projection for a server-supplied modeled semester input; there is no persisted/user-facing modeled plan projection yet, so the live endpoint does not claim any course is planned. Single-option verified prerequisite edges receive explicitly limited structural-impact annotation, not a guaranteed graduation critical path. Presentation provenance (plan number/effective year/plan-row update time) is read separately from the hash-pinned P6 progress model. The roadmap has state filters, search, keyboard-operable course buttons, a details region, and an accessible structured-list view; the report has print-to-PDF styling, source date, plan identity, and no graduation guarantee. Manifest/theme metadata use an existing approved icon and deliberately add no service worker or private offline cache. The API response omits owner selection and unnecessary internal source IDs; browser requests use `no-store`. Synthetic tests cover six states, OR-group criticality, missing/cross-plan data, new/repeat/near-complete profiles, and a 75-course plan. ## Production academic CPU admission and disconnect closure (2026-09-30)

**Phase/slice/objective and starting state:** At `main` HEAD `171132cb7ba248d197e34cc4ca0c127f6a00b220`, close the three pre-commit blockers only: Copilot 503/504 error loss, fragmented CPU admission including synchronous semester planners, and unproven disconnect watcher cleanup. The earlier degree-path optimization, timing, frontend work, and two untracked production SQL backups were preserved. No stage, commit, push, production deployment, or migration was performed.

**Implementation and security:** Added one process-local `AcademicComputeLimiter`, configured by `ACADEMIC_COMPUTE_MAX_CONCURRENT` (validated 1–4, conservative default 1), and injected the same instance into the application's student, advisor, and Advisor Copilot services. Admission is fail-fast with no queue; a slot is held until the shielded pure-CPU worker actually exits, including after caller cancellation or a 50-second deadline. Student/advisor/Copilot degree paths, Copilot What-If, and student/advisor/Copilot semester planners use this boundary. Async owner-scoped Supabase reads and advisor authorization remain on the event loop and outside the worker. Pure planners receive the same cooperative budget check; no partial result is returned after budget failure. Threads protect event-loop scheduling but do not provide true parallel Python CPU execution under the GIL. Copilot now maps the existing typed capacity/timeout exceptions to sanitized structured 503/504 errors before its generic 500 catch. The student degree-path watcher now awaits `Request.receive()` for `http.disconnect` and is cancelled and awaited when processing ends, avoiding Starlette's immediately-cancelled `is_disconnected()` polling scope that previously blocked cleanup. No credentials, student data, messages, or local keys were logged.

**Verification:** New low-level ASGI `http.disconnect` test proves cooperative worker stop, watcher termination, release, and a successful subsequent request. Cross-route tests prove one student degree-path job excludes Copilot degree path, Copilot semester planner, and Copilot What-If; worker success/error/timeout release is covered. Copilot HTTP tests prove degree-path and What-If 503/504 plus unexpected 500. Focused backend set: **222 passed**. A synthetic one-loop CPU HTTP probe measured health 0.843s, academic progress 0.679s, advisor preflight 0.136s, GENERAL_CHAT 0.399s, and degree path 1.289s. A separate real, read-only Local Supabase Plan 12 8-semester computation produced 3 paths in 11.548s, then released the slot. A combined real Plan 12 / mixed-route run returned 200/3 paths in 11.758s while Copilot degree path, planner, and What-If each returned controlled 503; starting all seven competing requests at once yielded health 1.700s, progress 1.495s, preflight 0.136s, and GENERAL_CHAT 1.166s. The sync health endpoint contributed under contention, so its pure handler was changed to async. A final real Plan 12 / lightweight-only overlap returned 200/3 paths in 12.363s with health 0.239s, progress 0.663s, preflight 0.119s, and GENERAL_CHAT 0.387s. Complete backend against this final code: **1,748 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped, 3 dependency deprecation warnings in 135.01s**. Frontend: **112 passed, 0 failed**; typecheck passed; lint 0 errors/45 existing warnings; production build passed. Local Supabase used temporary 553xx ports because Windows reserves defaults; it was stopped and `supabase/config.toml` restored byte-for-byte to HEAD after verification. `git diff --check` passed.

**This slice's changed files and bytes on disk:** `apps/api/app/core/academic_compute.py` 2,682 (new); `core/config.py` 1,059; `main.py` 23,989; `services/student.py` 14,013; `services/advisor.py` 15,887; `advisor/orchestrator.py` 29,617; `advisor_copilot/dispatcher.py` 8,531; `advisor_copilot/errors.py` 3,055; `advisor_copilot/adapters.py` 46,819; `api/routes/advisor_copilot.py` 2,172; `api/routes/student.py` 14,519; `api/routes/health.py` 405; `apps/api/tests/test_academic_compute.py` 4,796 (new); `test_advisor_copilot.py` 89,280; `test_degree_path_service.py` 17,533; `test_semester_planner_service.py` 10,929. This progress record is also changed and its final bytes/diff are verified at handoff.

**Acceptance / remaining risks / exact next starting point:** Locally verified for owner review. The limiter is process-local: multiple ASGI worker processes would each receive their own one-job capacity, so recheck if Render worker count changes. The in-process ASGI measurements are local evidence, not a live production latency claim. Mixed traffic still raised some lightweight latencies above one second with seven simultaneous calls before the health-handler change; post-change mixed-load saturation should be observed in production. Owner should review the full uncommitted diff before any separately approved stage/commit/deploy, then compare privacy-safe timings and saturation under real traffic. The two production SQL backup files remain untouched and untracked.

## Production degree-path event-loop starvation repair (2026-09-30)

**Phase/slice/objective and starting state:** At `main` HEAD `171132cb7ba248d197e34cc4ca0c127f6a00b220`, prove and repair single-worker request starvation during Plan 12 degree-path generation. The only initial untracked files were `production_pre_p8_replay_data.sql` and `production_pre_p8_replay_schema.sql`; neither was opened, changed, staged, or committed. No production resource, migration, deployment, push, or commit was touched.

**Diagnosis and before/after evidence:** A one-worker local ASGI run with real read-only Plan 12 catalogs and synthetic owner-scoped state reproduced the production pattern: an 8-semester degree-path request took 93.101s; requests started 0.5s later waited approximately 92.6s for health, academic progress, CORS preflight, and advisor general chat. Pure-engine profile for the same empty-history/15-credit/5-course/8-semester/3-path input took 98.999s: 22 states, 108,746 valid combinations, 98.287s in semester planning, including 34.837s repeated progress calculation and 53.747s repeated eligibility evaluation. This is CPU/combinatorial work executed synchronously on the ASGI event loop, not a blocking database query or an OpenAI call. After the repair the same pure input completed in 13.843s, with 12.874s semester planning, 1.703s progress projection, 6.239s eligibility, the same 22 expanded states, 108,746 combinations, and 3 paths. A new one-worker run returned health 200/0.334s, academic progress 200/0.414s, OPTIONS advisor 200/0.475s, and GENERAL_CHAT 200/0.576s **while degree path remained in flight**; degree path returned 200 in 13.111s.

**Implementation/security:** Request lifecycle logs now record random request ID, method, redacted path shape, UTC start/end, duration, and status; degree-path logs include auth (existing), student resolution, profile/attempt loads, catalog load, prerequisite graph mapping, remaining courses, baseline/affected eligibility, semester simulation, scenario generation, search, ranking, serialization, and total. Logs added here do not include tokens, headers, messages, student IDs, raw catalog rows, or secrets. The existing deterministic Phase 6 progress rules provide a validated baseline projection for hypothetical passed courses, with exhaustive small-catalog equivalence tests. A prerequisite option index limits authoritative Phase 5 reevaluation to targets that a selected course could unlock; beam width, ranking, ownership, tenant boundaries, RLS use, and academic decision authority are unchanged. Student degree paths and explanation graphs, advisor degree-path modeling, Advisor Copilot degree-path tool, and the What-If route's two Phase 9 calls now execute CPU search off the event loop with cooperative 50-second server budget, capacity guard, and typed 504/503 failures. The student HTTP route also signals cancellation on disconnect. Frontend GENERAL_CHAT pending copy is neutral; it does not claim deterministic work or fake backend stages. The client 55/60-second deadlines remain unchanged. This bounded thread approach improves event-loop scheduling after algorithmic optimization; it does not claim a Python thread removes all GIL contention. Render free-instance cold starts remain a separate possible 50-second latency source; no hosting plan changed. Real backend stage streaming/SSE is deferred.

**Verification and intermediate issues:** The first focused backend run had 32 fake-service signature failures after adding the optional cancellation argument; the fake was updated without weakening behavior. An initial Local Supabase Plan 12 test then stalled during awaited disconnect-watcher cleanup; removing that blocking await made the isolated real test pass (1 passed, 0 skipped, 2 dependency warnings in 48.59s). The final complete backend command from the repository root, `apps/api/.venv311/Scripts/python.exe -m pytest apps/api/tests -q -rs`, with process-only local API/Auth/DB credentials and Plan 12 ID: **1,735 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped, 3 dependency deprecation warnings in 137.26s**. Frontend `npm test`: **112 passed, 0 failed**; `npm run typecheck`: passed; `npm run lint`: 0 errors, 45 existing warnings; `npm run build`: passed. A service test proves deadline-triggered worker exit/capacity release and event-loop responsiveness; API tests prove safe 503/504 codes; progress tests compare projected and full Phase 6 results; a What-If test proves cancellation reaches the deterministic engines. Windows-reserved default Supabase ports required temporary local 553xx `supabase/config.toml` values for integration verification. Local Supabase was stopped and that file restored exactly to HEAD; the port change is not part of this work. No local database reset or production migration occurred.

**Changed files and actual byte sizes:** `apps/api/app/academic_digital_twin/engine.py` 11,384; `advisor/orchestrator.py` 29,527; `advisor_copilot/adapters.py` 47,324; `api/routes/student.py` 14,213; `catalog/supabase_repository.py` 30,476; `core/request_timing.py` 2,432 (new); `degree_path/engine.py` 38,080; `degree_path/models.py` 10,775; `main.py` 23,629; `planner/engine.py` 21,986; `progress/engine.py` 9,696; `services/advisor.py` 16,751; `services/student.py` 14,386; `student/supabase_repository.py` 23,093. `apps/api/tests/test_academic_digital_twin.py` 25,430; `test_degree_path_api.py` 26,701; `test_degree_path_service.py` 17,519; `test_progress_engine.py` 10,432; `test_request_timing.py` 1,726 (new). `apps/web/src/app/(student)/student/advisor/page.tsx` 12,968; `portal-pages.test.tsx` 35,869. ## Advisor general-chat safety and scoped request deadlines (2026-09-30)

**Phase/slice/objective:** final pre-commit repair of two review findings only: academic questions misrouted as `GENERAL_CHAT`, and the unintended shared 60-second browser deadline. **Starting SHA/state:** `main` at `65425b056ddd401d6f1986a73ce44ac531e52ca7`, matching `origin/main`; all prior policy/eligibility and advisor/degree-path edits remain uncommitted. The two untracked production replay SQL backups were not opened or changed. No production write, migration, stage, commit, push, or deploy.

**Implementation/security:** A deny-only, bilingual lightweight academic-domain guard checks messages classified `GENERAL_CHAT` for eligibility/registration, prerequisites, credits and completed courses, progress, planning, degree path, recommendations, university policy, and academic what-if cues. It reroutes a detected request into the existing normalized academic path, where missing entities or constraints yield clarification and supported facts use deterministic engines. It also rejects a general response containing an apparent student-specific academic claim, returning clarification without student data access. Genuine general chat still makes no student, catalog, policy, embedding, or engine call. The browser API client no longer imposes a global deadline. Degree-path POST explicitly passes a 60-second signal; advisor POST explicitly passes a 55-second signal. Degree-path distinguishes timeout Arabic copy from other failures, retains retry and `finally` cleanup, and never attributes a generic error to the student's record. Existing server-side AI and embedding HTTP timeouts remain 20 seconds. Browser abort does not cancel already-running backend CPU work; token acquisition/refresh is outside fetch cancellation and was not rewritten.

**Verification:** backend targeted advisor suite **231 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped, 2 dependency warnings**; current backend collection **1718**. The previous full suite's 1707/1707 result predates this guard and is not represented as a new full run. Frontend complete suite **111 passed**, targeted client/portal **36 passed**, typecheck passed, lint `--quiet` passed, production build passed. `git diff --check` passed; nothing staged. **Acceptance:** focused regression ready for owner review; no new full backend run. **Next starting point:** review the guard's conservative clarification tradeoff and the feature-specific browser deadlines; separately observe production timings after an approved deployment.

**Changed files and measured bytes:** `apps/api/app/services/advisor.py` 14648; `apps/api/tests/test_advisor_service.py` 20959; `apps/web/src/lib/api/authenticated-client.ts` 3131; `authenticated-client.test.ts` 6534; `apps/web/src/lib/api/student-api.ts` 9986; `apps/web/src/app/(student)/student/advisor/page.tsx` 13031; `degree-path/page.tsx` 21394; `portal-pages.test.tsx` 35271.

## Production student API diagnosis and policy-route repair (2026-09-29)

**Phase/slice/objective:** narrow production-safe repair of `GET /api/v1/me/policies` AttributeError, plus diagnosis of degree-path 405 and eligibility `1501332` 404. **Starting SHA/state:** `main` at `65425b056ddd401d6f1986a73ce44ac531e52ca7`, matching `origin/main`; pre-existing untracked `production_pre_p8_replay_data.sql` and `production_pre_p8_replay_schema.sql` were preserved without reading or modifying them. No production migration, write, deployment, stage, commit, or push occurred.

**Root cause and change:** all four policy handlers call `StudentService.resolve_student_university_id`, absent from the concrete service although the owner-scoped `SupabaseStudentAcademicRepository.resolve_student_university_id` already implements the exact relationship lookup. The policy tests previously substituted a dummy service that masked the mismatch. Added only a service delegation to the existing repository method; the route keeps its authenticated user ID, missing-profile behavior, and verified/current university-scoped policy service. Policy API and answer tests now inject the concrete `StudentService` with a fake repository. The real Local Supabase Auth/FastAPI test additionally verifies a profiled student's policy list and an unprofiled user's empty list. No new query, hardcoded tenant, RLS bypass, or migration was added.

**Other findings:** `POST /api/v1/me/degree-paths` is the documented on-demand, nonpersisted degree-path computation. The frontend `StudentApiService.createDegreePaths` explicitly POSTs, as does its graph call; a full frontend search found no GET caller. GET therefore correctly returns 405, and no route or frontend degree-path change was made. The eligibility service loads the authenticated student's plan, then an exact course code within that plan's university. Missing target course maps to `TARGET_NOT_FOUND`/404; a university course outside the plan maps to 409. A read-only query of the linked production catalog returned zero `courses` rows for `1501332` (also absent locally), so this specific 404 is consistent with missing catalog data, not a lookup bug. The UI had hardcoded, tenant-unaware clickable course suggestions including `1501332`; removed those suggestions while retaining manual entry and the exact server decision. No production catalog record was created.

**Tests and security:** final combined unit and real Local Supabase backend run on policy API/answer, student API, degree-path API, student/Auth integration, policy persistence, and policy answer: **113 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped, 3 existing deprecation warnings in 12.82s**. Frontend focused portal suite **18 passed / 1 file**; typecheck passed; targeted lint **0 errors, 4 pre-existing unused-symbol warnings**; production build passed. `git diff --check` passed with line-ending conversion warnings only. Owner/tenant boundary remains server-derived and verified/current policy filtering remains unchanged. **Acceptance:** local fix ready for owner review; production remains unfixed until separately approved code deployment and post-deploy verification. **Remaining uncertainty:** the provided degree-path GET log has no caller identity; the checked repository frontend does not issue it. No full backend suite was rerun for this narrow service delegation.

## WC-040 P6 exact historical replay — implementation checkpoint (2026-09-29)

**Starting state:** `main` at `6a98afc688ab97da56afcd190620d0f0d2247fc4`, equal to `origin/main`, clean. **Objective:** exact historical deterministic replay for new trusted P6 mock-registration submissions only. **Gap verified before implementation:** the immutable revision/outbox preserves selected codes, outcome, fingerprint, and version labels, but not the historical eligibility catalog/prerequisite groups, progress catalog/progress states, attempts, and period facts consumed by `validate_registration_intent`. The new wrapper RPC persists revision, course children, outbox, and artifact in one database transaction; idempotent retries require exact artifact equality and reject missing/conflicting artifacts. `P6_REPLAY_ARTIFACT_V1` canonically hashes the minimal validation-consumed intent, period, catalog/prerequisite, progress, attempts, version metadata, and original full deterministic output; it excludes generated retry-unstable intent IDs, GPA, names, secrets, prompts, and unrelated profile facts. ## WC-040 Decision Trace Ledger final closure assessment (2026-09-29)

**Phase/slice:** P8 WC-040 approved final contract. **Starting SHA/branch:** `095f46f8e2e9eaf9b3deec50e8b89269cfd5063b` / `main`, equal to `origin/main`, clean worktree. **Objective:** add only the assigned-advisor read surface and reconcile retention/export/replay/integrity/supersession and material-event exit evidence. **Implementation:** added authenticated GET-only `/api/v1/advisor/students/{student_user_id}/decision-history` list/detail, reusing the existing P7 advisor authorization service before scoped ledger reads. ## Advisor course-resolution catalog integrity repair (2026-09-28)

**Phase/slice:** Production-bug remediation limited to the Advisor course-resolution catalog. **Objective:** prevent valid unnamed `referenced_only` prerequisite identities outside a student's plan from breaking academic course questions. **Starting SHA/branch:** `dc0077f2728562dae0385bc33a5e9fbd7b6308e2` / `main`; working tree was clean. **Confirmed cause:** `load_advisor_course_catalog` queried every course in the study-plan university and required `name_ar` on all rows. The frontend was inspected but left untouched: `StudentApiService` currently maps backend 500 responses to generic `SERVER_ERROR` and does not expose the safe backend `error_code`/`detail` to this page. **Tests added:** repository regression for unnamed nonmember `referenced_only` and foreign-university courses, Arabic/English/code resolution, malformed actual members and duplicate codes; mocked-provider Arabic Advisor service regression using the real catalog adapter; read-only real Local Supabase advisor catalog regression. **Validation:** focused catalog/Advisor/API set: **123 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped, 2 warnings in 3.04s**; final fixture refinement: **39 passed in 0.46s**; full backend collection: **1,520**; full backend: **1,520 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped, 3 existing dependency warnings in 600.53s (10:00)**. ## Project identity and immutable architecture

- **Product:** Morshidi, an Arabic-first academic intelligence operating system.
- **Pilot:** Zarqa University, Artificial Intelligence, Study Plan 12.
- **Architectural invariant:** **AI EXPLAINS — DETERMINISTIC RULES DECIDE.**
- LLM/RAG may explain, summarize, retrieve governed policy evidence, and cite sources. They must not independently decide eligibility, prerequisites, progress, graduation, semester validity, or degree-path correctness.
- Student data remains owner-scoped. Advisor access requires the established active advisor role, same-university boundary, and active explicit student assignment. Analysts have no student-level trace access.

**Blocking prerequisite:** recover the six authoritative P8 companion policy/test-matrix documents from an approved external source (or have the product/security owner approve new policy authoring) and establish a real Git worktree. Do not invent their requirements.

**Then, and only after explicit human approval:** define and implement the smallest P8 Slice 2 persistence/security slice: a canonical trace/evidence schema and append-only service-side append boundary, with an explicit RLS/RPC access matrix.
